<!-- theorem--1: body_md -->
Let $D$ and $D'$ be decorated tangle diagrams with the same boundary data presenting the same oriented tangle $T$. Then $\mathcal M(D)$ and $\mathcal M(D')$ are 1-homotopy equivalent.
<!-- end theorem--1 -->
<!-- corollary--1: body_md -->
Let $\mathbf k$ be a field. The bifiltered twisted complex $\text{\CYRD}^\infty(T)$ over $\mathcal B_{ds}^\infty\otimes\mathbf k$ is an invariant of $T$, up to 1-homotopy equivalence.
<!-- end corollary--1 -->
<!-- question-1: body_md -->
Is there a tractable definition of an $A_\infty$-extension of $\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}(E_*^{2k})$ in terms of cobordisms, such that it is quasi-isomorphic to one induced from an extension of $\mathcal B(E^{2k}_*)$ coming from matrix factorizations?
<!-- end question-1 -->
<!-- question-2: body_md -->
Is there a formulation of the extended Bar-Natan invariant $\text{\CYRD}^\infty(T)$ without appeal to matrix factorizations, by adapting the definition of the Bar-Natan bracket ${[\![ T ]\!]}$ into one of a twisted complex over the distinguished extension of $\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}(E_*^{2k})$?
<!-- end question-2 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
Let $E$ be a finite set of points on the boundary circle of a 2-dimensional disk $\mathbb D^2$. The linearized cobordism category $\mathsf{Cob}^3(E)$ is the $\mathbb{Z}$-graded $\mathbb{Z}[G]$-linear category defined as follows.
	\begin{itemize}
		\item Objects are embedded 1-manifolds (crossingless tangles) $\Gamma\subseteq \mathbb D^2$ such that $\partial \Gamma=\Gamma\cap\partial \mathbb D^2=E$.
		\item Morphisms $\Gamma_0\to \Gamma_1$ are formal $\mathbb{Z}[G]$-linear combinations of dotted cobordisms between $\Gamma_0$ and $\Gamma_1$, embedded in $\mathbb D^2\times [0,1]$, and considered up to isotopy rel. boundary. By dotted, we mean that each component may carry a finite number of dots that can freely move along the component.
		\item Identities are given by product cobordisms; composition is by gluing.
		\item The (quantum) grading of a cobordism $C$ is defined to be $q(C):= \chi(C)-\frac12 |E|-2\#\mbox{dots}$.

	\end{itemize}
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
Let $E$ be a finite subset of the circle $\partial \mathbb D^2$. The \emph{Bar-Natan category} $\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}(E)$ is the category $\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}(E)=\Sigma\mathsf{Cob}^3(E)_{/\ell}$ obtained by quotienting with respect to the following local relations and taking additive enlargement.
	\begin{align*}
		\vcenter{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale=0.25]{figures/sphere}}}=0
		&& \vcenter{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale=0.25]{figures/cylinder}}} = \vcenter{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale=0.25]{figures/dottedcupcap}}}+\vcenter{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale=0.25]{figures/cupdottedcap}}}+G\vcenter{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale=0.25]{figures/cupcap}}}
		\\\\
		\vcenter{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale=0.25]{figures/dottedsphere}}}=1
		&& \vcenter{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale=0.25]{figures/doubledottedplane}}} \,+\, G\vcenter{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale=0.25]{figures/dottedplane}}}=0
	\end{align*}
	If $E_*$ is a pointed set with underlying set $E$ and basepoint $*\in E$, then we define $\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}(E_*)$ as a further quotient with respect to the additional relation
	\[
		\vcenter{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale=0.25]{figures/dottedbasedplane}}}=0,
	\]
	which asserts the vanishing of cobordisms with dots on the based component.
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
Let $E$ be a possibly pointed finite subset of the circle $\partial \mathbb D^2$. We define $\mathcal B(E)$
	to be the full subcategory of $\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}(E)$ with objects given by a full set of representatives of isotopy classes of crossingless matchings with boundary $E$.
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
The inclusion $\iota:\Sigma\mathcal B(E) \hookrightarrow \mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}(E)$ is an equivalence, and thus it induces an equivalence between $Ch(\Sigma\mathcal B(E))$ and $Ch (\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}(E))$.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
Let us write an explicit inverse equivalence. The \emph{delooping functor} $\mathbf{Del}:\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}(E)\to \Sigma\mathcal B(E)$ is defined on objects by
	\[
		\mathbf{Del}(\Gamma) := \bigoplus_{X\subseteq \mathrm{circ}(\Gamma)}q^{|\mathrm{circ}(\Gamma)|-2|X|}o(\Gamma).
	\]

	If $C:\Gamma_0\to \Gamma_1$ is a morphism between crossingless tangles, and $X\subseteq \mathrm{circ}(\Gamma_0)$, $Y\subseteq \mathrm{circ}(\Gamma_1)$, we define $\mathbf{Del}(C)$ component-wise by
	\[
		\mathbf{Del}(C)^{X,Y} := \rotatebox[origin=c]{180}{$\mathsf{D}$}_Y\circ C\circ \mathsf{D}_X.
	\]
	Here, $\mathsf{D}_X:o(\Gamma_0)\to \Gamma_0$ is a decorated cobordism consisting of a cylindrical cobordism in $o(\Gamma_0)$, caps bounding all loops in $\mathrm{circ}(\Gamma_0)$, and black dots on all caps bounding loops in $X$. Similarly, $\rotatebox[origin=c]{180}{$\mathsf{D}$}_Y:\Gamma_1\to o(\Gamma_1)$ is a decorated cobordism consisting of a cylinder in $o(\Gamma_1)$, cups bounding all loops in $\mathrm{circ}(\Gamma_1)$, and red dots on all cups bounding loops in $\mathrm{circ}(\Gamma_1)\setminus Y$. We have the identity $\mathbf{Del}\circ\iota=\mathrm{id}_{\Sigma\mathcal B(E)}$ on the nose, while
	\begin{align*}
		\phi_\Gamma:=
		&
		(\mathsf{D}_X)_{X\subseteq \mathrm{circ}(\Gamma)} : \bigoplus_{X\subseteq \mathrm{circ}(\Gamma)} q^{|\mathrm{circ}(\Gamma)|-2|X|}o(\Gamma) \longrightarrow \Gamma,
		\\
		\phi^{-1}_\Gamma:=
		&
		(\rotatebox[origin=c]{180}{$\mathsf{D}$}_Y)_{Y \subseteq \mathrm{circ}(\Gamma)}:
		\Gamma \longrightarrow
		\bigoplus_{Y\subseteq \mathrm{circ}(\Gamma)} q^{|\mathrm{circ}(\Gamma)|-2|Y|}o(\Gamma)
	\end{align*} give explicit, mutually inverse, natural isomorphisms $\iota\circ\mathbf{Del}\cong \mathrm{id}_{\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}(E)}$.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:basis}
	Let $\Gamma_0, \Gamma_1$ be crossingless tangles in $\mathbb D^2$ with the same boundary $E$. Let $\mathbf B$ be a basis for $\operatorname{hom}_{\mathcal B(E)}(o(\Gamma_0),o(\Gamma_1))$ over $\mathbb{Z}$. Then
	\[
		\left\{\mathsf{D}_Y\circ C\circ \rotatebox[origin=c]{180}{$\mathsf{D}$}_X\mid C\in\mathbf B,\, X\subseteq \mathrm{circ}(\Gamma_0),\, Y\subseteq \mathrm{circ}(\Gamma_1) \right\}
	\]
	is a basis for $\operatorname{hom}_{\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}(E)}(\Gamma_0,\Gamma_1)$ over $\mathbb{Z}$.
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
For an arbitrary oriented tangle diagram $D$ with boundary $E$ and crossings labeled by $1,\dots,n$, the \emph{Bar-Natan bracket} is the complex over $\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}(E)$ defined by
	\[
		[\![D]\!]:=\bigoplus_{s} q^{n_+-2n_-+|s|}h^{|s|-n_-}\Gamma_s,
	\]
	with indexing over all total resolutions $s\in\{0,1\}^n$. Here, given a total resolution $s$, we denote the number of 1-resolutions in $s$ by $|s|$, and the crossingless tangle obtained by applying all resolutions in $s$ to the diagram $D$ by $\Gamma_s$. The number of positive and negative crossings are denoted by $n_+$ and $n_-$, respectively. To define the differential, let us additionally denote the number of 1-resolutions among the crossings $1,\dots,i-1$ in $s$ by $|s|_{<i}$. The differential $d_{[\![D]\!]}$ has a non-zero component $s\to s'$ precisely when $s$ and $s'$ differ by a change from 0- to 1-resolution, in which case we write $s<_is'$. The precise expression is
	\[
		d_{[\![D]\!]} := \sum_{s<_is'} (-1)^{|s|_{<i}} S_{i},
	\]
	where $S_i$ is a cobordism given by a saddle at the location of the $i$-th crossing, extended by the identity everywhere else.
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
Let $\mathbf K$ be a commutative ring. An $A_\infty$\emph{-category} $\mathcal C$ over $\mathbf K$ consists of a set of objects $Ob(\mathcal C)$, a $\mathbb{Z}$-graded $\mathbf K$-module $\mathcal C(X,Y)$ for each $X,Y\in Ob(\mathcal C)$, and linear structure maps
	\[
		\mu_n:\mathcal C(X_{n-1},X_n)\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal C(X_0,X_1)\to \mathcal C(X_0,X_n)
	\]
	of degree $2-n$ for $n\geq 1$. We refer to $\mu_1$ as the \emph{differential}, $\mu_2$ as the \emph{composition}, and $\mu_n$ as the \emph{higher compositions} for $n\geq 3$. There are \emph{units} $1_X\in\mathcal C(X,X)$ which satisfy $\mu_2(1,a)=a$ and $\mu_2(a,1)=(-1)^{|a|}a$. Higher compositions vanish if any input is a unit. The structure maps satisfy the $A_\infty$-relations
	\[
		0 = \sum_{i+j+k=n} (-1)^\odot \mu_{i+1+k}(a_n,\dots,a_{n-i+1},\mu_j(a_{n-i},\dots,a_{k+1}),a_k,\dots,a_1)
	\]
	for all composable sequences $a_1,\dots,a_n$, where $\odot=|a_k|+\cdots +|a_1|+k$, and $|-|$ denotes the alluded $\mathbb{Z}$-grading. We call $|-|$ the $A_\infty$\emph{-grading}. An $A_\infty$-category is said to be $q$-filtered if there is an additional degree $q$ on each $\mathcal C(X,Y)$ such that the operations $\mu_n$ are homogeneous of degree $q(\mu_n)=3(n-2)$.
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- example-1: body_md -->
\label{ex:diskseq}
	Consider the quiver algebra
	\[
		\mathcal B:= \mathcal B(E^4_*) =
		\left[
		\begin{tikzcd}
			\circ
			\arrow[loop left, "D"]
			\arrow[r, bend left=20, "S"]
			&
			\bullet
			\arrow[loop right, "D"]
			\arrow[l, bend left=20, "S"]
		\end{tikzcd}
		\right]_
		{/(SD=0, DS=0)}.
	\]
	Following \cite{hkk}, we enhance $\mathcal B$ into an $A_\infty$-algebra $\mathcal B^\infty_{ds}$ over $\mathbb{Z}$ by defining an $A_\infty$-grading and higher operations, as follows. For the grading, we declare
	\begin{align*}
		|\circ \xrightarrow{S}\bullet|=|\bullet \xrightarrow{S}\circ| = |\bullet \xrightarrow{D}\bullet| = 0, \qquad |\circ \xrightarrow{D}\circ|=2.
	\end{align*}
	In order to describe the higher operations, we begin by recursively defining what it means for an even-length sequence of paths in $\mathcal B$ to be a \emph{disk sequence}. A \emph{disk sequence} of length 4 is a composable sequence of arrows of the form $(S,D,S,D)$ or $(D,S,D,S)$. If $(a_{2k},\dots, a_{1})$ is a \emph{disk sequence} of length $2k$, then any sequence of composable paths of the form $(a_{2k},\dots,a_iS,D,S,Da_{i-1},\dots, a_{1})$ with $a_iS\neq 0$ is a disk sequence of length $2k+2$, and so is any sequence of composable paths of the form $(a_{2k},\dots,a_iD,S,D,Sa_{i-1},\dots, a_{1})$ with $a_iD\neq 0$. A disk sequence is defined to be any sequence obtained by iterated application of this procedure. Define $\mu_{2k}^{\mathcal B^\infty_{ds}}:\mathcal B^{\otimes 2k}\to \mathcal B$, for $2k\geq 4$, by declaring
	\begin{align*}
		\mu_{2k}^{\mathcal B^\infty_{ds}} (a_{2k},\dots,a_1b)
		&
		:= b ,
		\\
		\mu_{2k}^{\mathcal B^\infty_{ds}} (ba_{2k},\dots,a_1)
		&
		:= b ,
	\end{align*}
	where $(a_{2k},\dots,a_{1})$ is a disk sequence and $b$ is any path such that $a_1b\neq 0$ (resp. $ba_{2k}\neq 0$). The operation $\mu_{2k}^{{\mathcal B}^\infty_{ds}}$ is also defined to vanish on any input sequence of composable paths that does not have either of the indicated forms. All odd operations $\mu_{2k+1}^{{\mathcal B}^\infty_{ds}}$ vanish identically. Observe that $\mathcal B^\infty_{ds}$ is $q$-filtered with respect to the usual quantum degree in the Bar-Natan category.
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
Given a bifiltered twisted complex $(\text{\CYRD}^\infty,\delta)\in Tw^h \mathcal B^\infty_{ds}$, there is always an underlying chain complex $(\text{\CYRD},\delta_1)\in Ch(\Sigma\mathcal B)$ obtained by forgetting the $\hslash$-grading and all components $\delta_k$ for $k>1$. In $(\text{\CYRD},\delta_1)$, the homological grading is still $h$, and the differential $\delta_1$ strictly preserves $q$-grading. We say that $\text{\CYRD}^\infty$ is an \emph{extension} of $\text{\CYRD}$.
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- example-3: body_md -->
Let $f:(\text{\CYRD}^\infty,\delta)\to(\tilde\text{\CYRD}^\infty,\tilde\delta)$ be a 1-homotopy equivalence in $Tw^h\mathcal B^\infty_{ds}$, witnessed by $(g_l,g_r,\kappa_l,\kappa_r)$. Then the map $f_0:(\text{\CYRD},\delta_1)\to(\tilde\text{\CYRD},\tilde\delta_1)$ between underlying chain complexes is an ordinary chain homotopy equivalence, witnessed by $((g_l)_0,(g_r)_0,(\kappa_l)_{-1},(\kappa_r)_{-1})$.
<!-- end example-3 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
Given a unital ($q$-filtered) $A_\infty$-functor $\mathcal F:\mathcal C\to \mathcal C'$, there are induced $A_\infty$-functors at the level of additive enlargements, twisted complexes and bifiltered twisted complexes. These induced functors preserve the notions of ($n$-)homomorphism, ($n$-)homotopy and ($n$-)homotopy equivalence.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:barnatanainfinity}
	Let $E$ be a finite subset of the circle $\partial \mathbb D^2$. Assume there are higher operations $(\mu^{\mathcal B^\infty(E)}_n)_{n\geq 3}$ and a grading defining a minimal $A_\infty$-extension $\mathcal B^\infty(E)$ of $\mathcal B(E)$. In other words, $\mu^{\mathcal B^\infty(E)}_1$ vanishes and $\mu^{\mathcal B^\infty(E)}_2$ is the usual multiplication in $\mathcal B(E)$. Then, there is a unique minimal $A_\infty$-extension $\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}^\infty(E)$ of $\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}(E)$ so that $\mathbf{Del}$ trivially extends into an $A_\infty$-functor $\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}^\infty(E)\to \Sigma \mathcal B^\infty(E)$. In the basis of Corollary \ref{cor:basis}, these higher operations are explicitly given by
	\[
		\mu_n^{\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}^\infty(E)}(\mathsf{D}_{Y_n}\circ C_n\circ \rotatebox[origin=c]{180}{$\mathsf{D}$}_{X_n},\dots,\mathsf{D}_{Y_1}\circ C_1\circ \rotatebox[origin=c]{180}{$\mathsf{D}$}_{X_1})
		=
		\begin{cases}
			\mathsf{D}_{Y_n}\circ\mu_n^{\mathcal B^\infty(E)}(C_n,\dots,C_1)\circ\rotatebox[origin=c]{180}{$\mathsf{D}$}_{X_1}, & \mbox{if every } Y_i=X_{i+1},
			\\
			0, & \mbox{otherwise.}
		\end{cases}
	\]
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Unraveling the desired functoriality condition, we need $\mu_n^{\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}^\infty(E)}$ to satisfy that, given any sequence of composable cobordisms $C_1,\dots,C_n$ with $C_i:\Gamma_{i-1}\to\Gamma_i$,
	\[
		\mathbf{Del}\left(\mu_n^{\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}^\infty(E)}(C_n,\dots,C_1)\right)
		= \mu_n^{\Sigma\mathcal B^\infty(E)}\left(\mathbf{Del}(C_n),\dots,\mathbf{Del}(C_1)\right).
	\]
	This forces
	\[
		\mu_n^{\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}^\infty(E)}(C_n,\dots,C_1)
		= \phi_{\Gamma_n}\circ\mu_n^{\Sigma\mathcal B^\infty(E)}\left(\phi^{-1}_{\Gamma_n}\circ C_n\circ \phi_{\Gamma_{n-1}},\dots,\phi^{-1}_{\Gamma_1}\circ C_1\circ \phi_{\Gamma_0}\right)\circ\phi^{-1}_{\Gamma_0}.
	\]
	Taking this equation as the definition of higher operations in $\mathsf{Cob}_{\ell}^\infty(E)$, one obtains the structure of an $A_\infty$-category, with the $A_\infty$-relations following directly from those in $\Sigma\mathcal B^\infty(E)$. The $A_\infty$-grading is also inherited from $\Sigma\mathcal B^\infty(E)$. The basis expression for these operations is readily verified, making use of the observation that
	\[
		(\phi^{-1}_{\Gamma'}\circ\mathsf{D}_{Y'}\circ C \circ \rotatebox[origin=c]{180}{$\mathsf{D}$}_{X'}\circ\phi_{\Gamma})^{X,Y} = \rotatebox[origin=c]{180}{$\mathsf{D}$}_Y\circ\mathsf{D}_{Y'}\circ C \circ \rotatebox[origin=c]{180}{$\mathsf{D}$}_{X'}\circ\mathsf{D}_X
		=\begin{cases}
			C, & \mbox{if } X'=X \mbox{ and } Y'=Y,
			\\
			0, & \mbox{otherwise}.
		\end{cases}
	\]
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
Let $R$ be a polynomial ring over $\mathbb{Z}$. A \emph{matrix factorization} over $R$ with potential $W\in R$ is a $\mathbb{Z}/2$-graded free $R$-module $M$ together with a degree 1 $R$-module homomorphism $d_M$ such that $d_M^2=W\cdot\mathrm{id}$. If $R$ and $M$ carry additional gradings, we require $d_M$ to be homogeneous with respect to these additional gradings.
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
Let $M$ and $N$ be matrix factorizations over $R$ with potential $W$. A \emph{morphism} $f: M\to N$ is a map of $R$-modules $f:M\to N$ such that $ fd_M=(-1)^{\hslash(f)}d_N f$. If it has $\hslash$-degree 0, we also call it a \emph{homomorphism}. We keep referring to maps $f: M\to N$ of the underlying $R$-modules simply as \emph{maps}.

	A \emph{homotopy} $\kappa: M\to N$ between homomorphisms $f,g:M\to N$ is an $\hslash$-degree 1 map such that $\kappa d_M+d_N\kappa =f-g$. We write $f\sim g$ when there is a homotopy between $f$ and $g$. Note that we require homomorphisms to preserve additional gradings $\mathfrak{gr}$, and homotopies to be homogeneous of degree $\mathfrak{gr}(\kappa)=-\mathfrak{gr}(d_M)$.

	A homomorphism $f: M\to N$ is said to be a \emph{homotopy equivalence} if it is invertible up to homotopy on both sides, i.e. if there exist homomorphisms $g_l, g_r: N\to M$ such that $fg_r\sim 1_N$ and $g_lf\sim1_M$.
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
Let $M$ and $N$ be matrix factorizations over $R$ with potential $W$, let $\iota:M\to N$ and $\rho:N\to M$ be homomorphisms, and let $\kappa$ be a homotopy between $\iota\rho$ and $1_N$. We say that $(M,N,\iota,\rho,\kappa)$ is a \emph{special deformation retract} if the maps satisfy the additional conditions $\rho\iota=1_M$, $\rho\kappa=0$, $\kappa\iota=0$ and $\kappa^2=0$.
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:fermioniccalculusformulas} The following identities of operators hold.
	\begin{align*}
		\theta_i\theta_j
		&
		= -\theta_j\theta_i,
		&
		\partial_i\partial_j
		&
		= -\partial_j\partial_i,
		\\
		\partial_i\partial_i
		&
		= 0,
		&
		\theta_i\theta_i
		&
		= 0,
		\\
		\partial_i\theta_i+\theta_i\partial_i
		&
		= 1 ,
		&
		\partial_i\theta_j
		&
		= -\theta_j\partial_i \quad\mbox{if } i\neq j,
		\\
		\partial_i\theta_i\partial_i
		&
		=\partial_i,
		&
		\theta_i\partial_i\theta_i
		&
		=\theta_i .
	\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
Let $\lambda\in R$ and denote the standard $i$-th unit vector by $\mathbf{\overline e}_i$. The following are isomorphisms of matrix factorizations.
	\begin{align*}
		l_{i,j}(\lambda) = 1+\lambda\partial_{i}\partial_{j}:
	K_{R}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a} &\mathbf{\overline b} 
		\end{pmatrix}
	}

		& \longrightarrow
		
	K_{R}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a}-\lambda b_i\mathbf{\overline e}_j+\lambda b_j\mathbf{\overline e}_i & \mathbf{\overline b} 
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
,
		\\
		r_{i,j}(\lambda) = 1+\lambda{\theta_i\theta_j}:
	K_{R}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a} &\mathbf{\overline b} 
		\end{pmatrix}
	}

		& \longrightarrow
		
	K_{R}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a} & \mathbf{\overline b} -\lambda a_i\mathbf{\overline e}_j+\lambda a_j\mathbf{\overline e}_i
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
,
		\\
		m_{i,j}(\lambda) = 1+\lambda \theta_i\partial_{j}:
	K_{R}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a} & \mathbf{\overline b} 
		\end{pmatrix}
	}

		& \longrightarrow
		
	K_{R}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a}-\lambda a_i\mathbf{\overline e}_j & \mathbf{\overline b}+\lambda b_j\mathbf{\overline e}_i 
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
,
		\\
		s_i = \partial_i\theta_i - \theta_i\partial_i:
	K_{R}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a} &\mathbf{\overline b} 
		\end{pmatrix}
	}

		& \longrightarrow
		
	K_{R}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a}-2a_i\mathbf{\overline e}_i & \mathbf{\overline b} -2b_i\mathbf{\overline e}_i
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
.
	\end{align*}
	In the first three isomorphisms, we require $i\neq j$ and $\lambda$ to be homogeneous of suitable degree to make the isomorphism itself homogeneous.
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
First, we verify that $l_{i,j}(\lambda)$ is a morphism of matrix factorizations by checking
	\[
		l_{i,j}(\lambda)\sum_k (a_k\partial_k+b_k \theta_k)
		=
		\left(\lambda(b_j\partial_i-b_i\partial_j)+\sum_k(a_k\partial_k+b_k \theta_k)\right)l_{i,j}(\lambda)
	\]
	Since $l_{i,j}(\lambda)=1+\lambda\partial_{i}\partial_{j}$ commutes with $\sum_{k\neq i,j} (a_k\partial_k+b_k \theta_k)$, we only need to check that
	\[
		(1+\lambda\partial_{i}\partial_{j})
		\left(a_i\partial_i+b_i\theta_i+a_j\partial_j +b_j\theta_j\right)
		=
		\left((a_i+\lambda b_j)\partial_i+b_i\theta_i+(a_j-\lambda b_i)\partial_j +b_j\theta_j\right)
		(1+\lambda\partial_{i}\partial_{j}).
	\]
	After distributing and canceling out terms appearing on both sides, as well as terms that involve $\partial_i^2$ or $\partial_j^2$, the equation boils down to
	\[
		b_i\lambda\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\theta_i+b_j\lambda\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\theta_j
		=
		b_j\lambda \partial_i - b_i\lambda \partial_j
		+b_i\lambda\theta_i\partial_{i}\partial_{j}+b_j\lambda\theta_j\partial_{i}\partial_{j}
		.
	\]
	This last identity is a consequence of the equation $1=\theta_k\partial_k+\partial_k\theta_k$. To prove that $l_{i,j}(\lambda)$ is an isomorphism, we observe that $l_{i,j}(\lambda)^{-1}=l_{i,j}(-\lambda)$. The cases of $m_{i,j}$ and $r_{i,j}$ are completely analogous.

	For $s_i$, we want to verify
	\[
		s_i\sum_k (a_k\partial_k+b_k \theta_k)
		=
		\left(-a_i\partial_i-b_i\theta_i+\sum_{k\neq i}(a_k\partial_k+b_k \theta_k)\right)s_i
	\]
	Since $s_i=\partial_i\theta_i - \theta_i\partial_i$ commutes with $\sum_{k\neq i} (a_k\partial_k+b_k \theta_k)$, the verification reduces to checking
	\[
		(\partial_i\theta_i - \theta_i\partial_i)
		\left(a_i\partial_i+b_i\theta_i\right)
		=
		\left(-a_i\partial_i-b_i\theta_i\right)
		(\partial_i\theta_i - \theta_i\partial_i).
	\]
	But from the identities in Lemma \ref{lem:fermioniccalculusformulas} it follows that both sides are equal to $a_i\partial_i-b_i\theta_i$.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:labelperm}
	Let $\mathbf{\overline a}'$ and $\mathbf{\overline b}'$ be obtained from $\mathbf{\overline a}$ and $\mathbf{\overline b}$ respectively by swapping the $i$-th and $j$-th entries: $a_i\leftrightarrow a_j$ and $b_i\leftrightarrow b_j$. Then we have a swap homomorphism $\tau_{i,j}:
	K_{R}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a} &\mathbf{\overline b} 
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
 \to 
	K_{R}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a}' & \mathbf{\overline b}' 
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
$ of the form
	\begin{align*}
		\tau_{i,j}
		&= \theta_i\partial_j+\theta_j\partial_i + \theta_j\theta_i\partial_j\partial_i + \partial_j\partial_i\theta_i\theta_j.
	\end{align*}
	It satisfies $\tau_{i,j}\tau_{i,j}=1$.
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
It follows by using the identities in Lemma \ref{lem:fermioniccalculusformulas} to verify that $\tau_{i,j}$ intertwines $\partial_i$ with $\partial_j$ and $\theta_i$ with $\theta_j$, and so it must intertwine the differentials. The involution property is also a consequence of these identities.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
A \emph{decorated diagram} is a crossingless tangle $\Gamma$ in the 2-dimensional disk $\mathbb D^2$ together with the following decorations.
	\begin{itemize}
		\item A checkerboard coloring $\sigma$ of the complement $\mathbb D^2\setminus\Gamma$.
		\item A finite indexed set $A=(\alpha_i)_{i\in I\subseteq \mathbb{N}}$ of non-intersecting directed arcs in the interior of the disk such that $\alpha_i\cap\Gamma=\partial \alpha_i$ transversally and $\Gamma\cup \bigcup_i\alpha_i$ is connected. We call $A$ an \emph{arc system}.
		\item A possible basepoint in $\partial \Gamma$, in which case we say that $\Gamma$ has a \emph{distinguished end}. We may write $\Gamma^*$ to emphasize the presence of a distinguished end.
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
Let $\Gamma$ be a decorated diagram with arc system $A$, and let $A'\subseteq A$ be a minimal connecting subset. Write $X_\alpha$ for the linear term in the matrix factorization associated to the arc $\alpha$, $x_c$ for the edge variable of a chosen edge in a loop $c \in \mathrm{circ}(\Gamma)$, and $x_0,\dots,x_{2k-1}$ for the boundary variables. If $\Gamma$ has no distinguished end, then
	\[
		R_{\Gamma} \cong \mathbb{Z}[G,X_\alpha,x_c, x_{i} \mid \alpha\in A\setminus A', c\in\mathrm{circ}(\Gamma), 0\leq i \leq 2k-2 ].
	\]
	If $\Gamma=\Gamma^*$ has a distinguished end, and $x_0$ is the distinguished variable, then
	\[
		R_{\Gamma^*} \cong \mathbb{Z}[G,X_\alpha,x_c, x_{i} \mid \alpha\in A\setminus A', c\in\mathrm{circ}(\Gamma), 1\leq i \leq 2k-2 ].
	\]
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- example-4: body_md -->

	We illustrate the labeling scheme with the following diagram. Take the minimal connecting set $A'=\{\alpha_1,\alpha_2\}$, so that, in principle, we can express $R_\Gamma\cong \mathbb{Z}[G,x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3,x_4,X_{\alpha_3}]$.

			($(-135:0.3)$) -- ($(45:0.3)$);

		\fi

[\mathbb{Z}][90*\arcthreedir][\arcthreelabel][\the\numexpr \iscrossingthree + \isemptythree \relax]

		\node[anchor=center] at (0.85,0.85) {\qdeg};
		\node[anchor=center] at (0.85,-0.85) {\hdeg};

	\end{tikzpicture}
}\qquad
		\begin{cases}
			x_5=x_0+x_2+x_4-x_1-x_3 \\
			u=X_{\alpha_3}+x_1\\
			v=x_2+x_4-x_3+X_{\alpha_3}\\
			w=x_2+X_{\alpha_3}
		\end{cases}
	\end{align*}
	The first diagram is fully labeled, while the second uses all possible implicit conventions. In practice, we favor the use of the indicated variable $u$ rather than $X_{\alpha_3}$ when doing algebraic manipulations with the explicit terms of the matrix factorization. In other words, we use the parametrization $R_\Gamma\cong \mathbb{Z}[G,x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3,x_4,u]$. In these variables, the associated matrix factorization is
	\begin{align*}
		\mathcal M (\Gamma)&=
	K_{R_\Gamma}{\begin{pmatrix}
			x_0-u & (x_1+x_3-x_2-x_4)(u+x_0+x_2+x_4-x_3-x_1+G) \\
			x_3-x_4& (u+x_2-x_1-x_3)(u+x_2+x_4-x_1+G) \\
			u-x_1& (x_1-x_2)(u+x_2+G)
		
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
 q^3\hslash
	\end{align*}
	with potential $W=\sum_{i=0}^{5}(-1)^i\left(\frac13 x_i^3+\frac G 2x_i^2\right).$
<!-- end example-4 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
\label{remarkalteration}
	Alterations of a labeled decorated diagram $\Gamma$, such as coloring swap, arrow orientation reversal or relabelings of edges or arcs, have easily described algebraic counterparts. Let us think of $\mathcal M(\Gamma)$ as a polynomial algebra $\mathcal M(\Gamma)=\mathbb{Z}[G,x_i,\vartheta_j]_{i,j}$, where $G$ and $x_i$ are even and $\vartheta_j$ are odd, and similarly $\mathcal M(\Gamma')=\mathbb{Z}[G,x'_k,\vartheta'_l]_{k,l}$ for the altered diagram $\Gamma'$. Each alteration induces an algebra isomorphism $\mathcal M(\Gamma)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal M(\Gamma')$:
	\begin{itemize}
		\item a coloring swap flips the sign of all even variables, including $G$,
		\item reversing orientation of the arc $\alpha_i$ flips the sign of $\vartheta_i$, and
		\item relabeling the edges or arcs maps the corresponding $x_i$ or $\vartheta_j$ to their respective $x_k'$ or $\vartheta_l'$.
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:mfdelooping}
	Given a decorated diagram $\Gamma$, there exists an explicit special deformation retract
	\[
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6, ampersand replacement=\&]
		{\bigoplus_{X\subseteq \mathrm{circ}(\Gamma)} \mathcal M(\Gamma')q^{|\mathrm{circ}(\Gamma)|-2|X|}\hslash^{|\mathrm{circ}(\Gamma)|}}
		\arrow[r, "{}", shift left=0.5ex, color=violet]
		\arrow[r, leftarrow, "{}", shift right=0.5ex, swap, color=violet]
		\& \mathcal M(\Gamma) \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{}",swap, color=violet]
	\end{tikzcd} 

	\]
	over $R_\Gamma^\partial$ such that each $\Gamma'$ is the crossingless matching $o(\Gamma)$ with any minimal set of connecting arcs.
<!-- end proposition-1 -->

	If the direction of the first (resp. second) arc on each diagram is flipped, then the formulas for the left-to-right and right-to-left isomorphisms are exchanged. The formulas remain the same if both arc directions are flipped in each diagram.

			arc (45:-45:0.35);
		\fi

		\end{tikzcd}.
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{sdrlemma} Let $R$ be a polynomial ring over $\mathbb{Z}$, let $u$ be a variable in quantum degree $q^{-2}$, and let $f\in R[u]$ be a polynomial of quantum degree $q^{-2n}$ with leading term $\pm u^n$. Let $g\in R[u]$ have quantum degree $q^{-2m}$. Let $M$ be a matrix factorization over $R[u]$ with potential of the form $W=\overline W-fg\in R[u]$, such that $\overline W\in R$. Denote
	\begin{align*}
		M'
		&
		:= M/fM
		,
		\\
		\overline M
		&
		:=
		
	K_{R[u]}{\begin{pmatrix}
			f & g
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
\otimes_{R[u]} M
		,
	\end{align*}
	thought of as matrix factorizations over $R$ with potential $\overline W$.
	Then there is a special deformation retract
	\[
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6, ampersand replacement=\&]
		{M'}
		\arrow[r, "{\iota}", shift left=0.5ex, color=violet]
		\arrow[r, leftarrow, "{\rho}", shift right=0.5ex, swap, color=violet]
		\& \overline M \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{\kappa}",swap, color=violet]
	\end{tikzcd} 

	\]
	of the form
	\[
		\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large, ]
			&
			M/fM
			\arrow[dd, shift right=0.6ex, "\iota_v", swap]
			\arrow[dd, leftarrow, shift left=0.6ex, "\rho"]
			\arrow[ddl, "\iota_d", bend right=20, swap]
			\\
			\\
			q^{m-n}\hslash M
			\arrow[r, "f", shift left=1ex, bend left=20, start anchor=east, end anchor=west]
			&
			M\arrow[l, "\kappa"]
			\arrow[l, "g", shift left=1ex, bend left=20, start anchor=west, end anchor=east].
		\end{tikzcd}
	\]

	The arrows are as follows:
	\begin{itemize}
		\item $\rho$ is the usual projection.
		\item $\kappa=-\operatorname{Quo}^u_f$.
		\item $\iota$ consists of a vertical component $\iota_v=\operatorname{Res}^u_f$ and a diagonal component $\iota_d=-\operatorname{Quo}^u_f\circ d_{M}\circ \iota_v$.
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
The identities $\kappa\iota=0$, $\rho\kappa=0$, $\rho\iota=1$ and $\kappa^2=0$ are straightforward.
	The identity
	\[
		d_{\overline M}\kappa+\kappa d_{\overline M} +1_{\overline M}=\iota\rho
	\]
	is verified as follows. Take $(x,z)\in q^{m-n}\hslash M \oplus M=\overline M$. We compute directly, keeping in mind that $\operatorname{Res}^u_f(z)=z-f\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(z)$ and $\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(fz)=z$:
	\begin{align*}
		(d_{\overline M}\kappa+\kappa d_{\overline M} +1_{\overline M})(x,z)
		&
		=(-\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(fx)+x -\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_M(z))-d_M(-\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(z)), -f\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(z) +z)
		\\
		&
		=(-\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_M(z))+d_M(\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(z)), \operatorname{Res}^u_f(z))
		,
	\end{align*}
	while
	\begin{align*}
		\iota\rho(x,z)
		&
		=(\iota_d([z]), \iota_v([z]))
		=(-\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_M(\operatorname{Res}^u_f(z))),\operatorname{Res}^u_f(z))
		\\
		&
		=(-\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_M(z-f\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(z))),\operatorname{Res}^u_f(z))
		\\
		&
		=(-\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_M(z))+d_M(\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(z)),\operatorname{Res}^u_f(z))
		.
	\end{align*}
	It is straightforward to verify that $\rho$ is a morphism. In the case of $\iota$, we have
	\begin{align*}
		\iota(d_{M'}([x]))
		&
		=( -\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_M^2(x))+d_M(\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_{M}(x))), \operatorname{Res}^u_f(d_{M}(x)))
		\\
		&
		=(d_M(\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_{M}(x))) -\operatorname{Quo}^u_f((\overline W -fg)x), \operatorname{Res}^u_f(d_{M}(x)))
		,
	\end{align*}
	and
	\begin{align*}
		d_{\overline M}(\iota([x]))
		&
		=d_{\overline M}(-\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_M(x))+d_M(\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(x)),\operatorname{Res}^u_f(x))
		\\
		&
		=
		(-d_M(-\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_M(x)))-d^2_M(\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(x))+g\operatorname{Res}^u_f(x),
		\\
		&
		\qquad\qquad d_M(\operatorname{Res}^u_f(x))-f\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_M(x))+fd_M(\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(x)))
		\\
		&
		=
		(d_M(\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_M(x)))-(\overline W -fg)\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(x)+g(x-f\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(x)),
		\\
		&
		\qquad\qquad d_M(\operatorname{Res}^u_f(x)+f\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(x))-f\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_M(x)))
		\\
		&
		=
		(d_M(\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_M(x)))-\overline W\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(x)+gx, d_M(x)-f\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_M(x)))
		\\
		&
		=(d_M(\operatorname{Quo}^u_f(d_{M}(x))) -\operatorname{Quo}^u_f((\overline W -fg)x), \operatorname{Res}^u_f(d_{M}(x)))
		.
	\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
\label{sdrremark}
	There is a slight variation in which $f$ appears in the second column, i.e.
	\[
		\overline M
		=
		
	K_{R[u]}{\begin{pmatrix}
			g & f
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
\otimes_{R[u]} M.
	\]
	In this case, we have $M'=q^{n-m}\hslash M/fM$, and the special deformation retract has the form
	\[
		\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large, ]
			q^{n-m}\hslash M/fM
			\arrow[dd, shift right=0.6ex, "\iota_v", swap]
			\arrow[dd, leftarrow, shift left=0.6ex, "\rho"]
			\arrow[ddr, "\iota_d", bend left=20, swap]
			&
			\\
			\\
			q^{n-m}\hslash M
			\arrow[r, "g", shift left=1ex, bend left=20, start anchor=east, end anchor=west]
			\arrow[r, "\kappa", swap]
			&
			M
			\arrow[l, "f", shift left=1ex, bend left=20, start anchor=west, end anchor=east],
		\end{tikzcd}
	\]
	The formulas for $\kappa$ and $\iota_v$ are the same as in the lemma, while the formula for $\iota_d$ switches sign.
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lemma:sdrkoszullinear}
	Let $R$ be a polynomial ring over $\mathbb{Z}$ and let $
	K_{R[u]}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a} &\mathbf{\overline b}
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
$ be a Koszul matrix factorization over $R[u]$ with $a_j=\pm u-c$, $\deg_u(c)=0$. Let $\mathbf{\overline a '}$ and $\mathbf{\overline b '}$ be the sequences obtained from $\mathbf{\overline a}$ and $\mathbf{\overline b}$ by deleting $a_j$ and $b_j$, respectively, and then setting $u=\pm c$. Then
	we have a special deformation retract
	\[
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6, ampersand replacement=\&]
		{
	K_{R}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\left.\mathbf{\overline a'}\right.
				&
				\left.\mathbf{\overline b'}\right.
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
}
		\arrow[r, "{\sum_{i=1}^r\left(\operatorname{Quo}_{a_j}^u(a_i)\partial_i + \operatorname{Quo}^u_{a_j}(b_i)\theta_i\right)\theta_j}", shift left=0.5ex, color=violet]
		\arrow[r, leftarrow, "{\operatorname{ev}_{u=\pm c}\partial_j\theta_j}", shift right=0.5ex, swap, color=violet]
		\& 
	K_{R[u]}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a}
				&\mathbf{\overline b}
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
 \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{-\operatorname{Quo}_{a_j}^u \theta_j}",swap, color=violet]
	\end{tikzcd} 

		[5cm]
	\]
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
First assume $j=1$, in which case Lemma \ref{sdrlemma} applies directly. The formulas $\kappa=-\operatorname{Quo}_{a_j}\theta_1$ and $\rho=\operatorname{ev}_{u=\pm c}\partial_1\theta_1$ follow easily, while the verification of $\iota=\sum_{i=1}^r\left(\operatorname{Quo}^u_{a_1}(a_i)\partial_i + \operatorname{Quo}^u_{a_1}(b_i)\theta_i\right)\theta_1$ is left to the reader. The case $j\neq 1$ follows from the case $j=1$ by conjugating everywhere by swap isomorphisms $\tau_{1,j}$, which has the effect of interchanging $\theta_1\leftrightarrow \theta_j$ and $\partial_1\leftrightarrow \partial_j$.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lemma:sdrkoszul} Let $R$ be a polynomial ring over $\mathbb{Z}$ and let $
	K_{R[u]}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a} &\mathbf{\overline b}
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
$ be a Koszul matrix factorization over $R[u]$ such that $b_j$ has leading term $\pm u^n$ with respect to $u$, and satisfies $\deg_u(\operatorname{Res}^u_{b_j}(a_i))=\deg_u(\operatorname{Res}^u_{b_j}(b_i))=0$ for all $i\neq j$. Let $\mathbf{\overline a '}$ and $\mathbf{\overline b '}$ be the sequences obtained from $\mathbf{\overline a}$ and $\mathbf{\overline b}$ by deleting $a_j$ and $b_j$ and then replacing $a_i$ and $b_i$, respectively, by $\operatorname{Res}^u_{b_j}(a_i)$ and $\operatorname{Res}^u_{b_j}(b_i)$ for $i\neq j$. Then we have a special deformation retract
	\[
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6, ampersand replacement=\&]
		{ \displaystyle\bigoplus_{l=0}^{n-1}
	K_{R}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\left.-\mathbf{\overline a'}\right.
				& \left.-\mathbf{\overline b'}\right.
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
q^{N-2l}\hslash}
		\arrow[r, "{\left(u^l\sum_{i=1}^r\left(\operatorname{Quo}_{b_j}^u(a_i)\partial_i + \operatorname{Quo}_{b_j}^u(b_i)\theta_i\right)\right)_l}", shift left=0.5ex, color=violet]
		\arrow[r, leftarrow, "{\left(P^{u,l}_{b_j}\partial_j\right)_l}", shift right=0.5ex, swap, color=violet]
		\& 
	K_{R[u]}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a} &\mathbf{\overline b}
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
 \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{-\operatorname{Quo}_{b_j}^u \partial_j}",swap, color=violet]
	\end{tikzcd} 

		[5.5cm],
	\]
	where $N=q(\vartheta_j)$.
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Similarly to the linear case, we apply Remark \ref{sdrremark} to obtain
	\[
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6, ampersand replacement=\&]
		{q^{N}\hslash M/fM}
		\arrow[r, "{\sum_{i=1}^r\left(\operatorname{Quo}_{b_j}^u(a_i)\partial_i + \operatorname{Quo}_{b_j}^u(b_i)\theta_i\right)}", shift left=0.5ex, color=violet]
		\arrow[r, leftarrow, "{\partial_j}", shift right=0.5ex, swap, color=violet]
		\& M \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{-\operatorname{Quo}_{b_j}^u \partial_j}",swap, color=violet]
	\end{tikzcd} 

		[5cm]
	\]
	for $M:=
	K_{R[u]}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a} &\mathbf{\overline b}
		\end{pmatrix}
	}
$. The map
	\[
		\displaystyle\bigoplus_{l=0}^{n-1}q^{-2l}
	K_{R}{\begin{pmatrix}
			\mathbf{\overline a'} & \mathbf{\overline b'}
		\end{pmatrix}
	}

		\xrightarrow{(u^l)_l}
		M/fM
	\]
	is an isomorphism of $R$-modules; its inverse is given by $(P_{b_j}^{u,l})_l$, and the condition $\deg_u(\operatorname{Res}^u_{b_j}(a_i))=\deg_u(\operatorname{Res}^u_{b_j}(b_i))=0$ for all $i\neq j$ guarantees that it is in fact a morphism of matrix factorizations. The special deformation retract in the statement of the lemma is obtained by composing.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- example-5: body_md -->
\label{ex:R1arcremoval}
	A first example is the removal of an arc connecting a component to itself
	\[
		
	\begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(current bounding box.center)]

				.. controls (-135:0.6) and (-0.4,-0.5) .. (-0.5,-0.65)
				-- (-1, -1) -- (-1, 1) -- cycle;

[\Y][90*\arconedir][\arconelabel][\the\numexpr \iscrossingone + \isemptyone \relax]

		\node[anchor=center] at (0.7,0.7) {\qdeg};

= 
	K_{R^\partial[u]}{\begin{pmatrix}
			u-x_0 & 0
		\end{pmatrix}
	}

	\]
	The boundary ring in this case is simply $R^\partial=\mathbb{Z}[G,x_0]$. The special deformation retract is
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:sdrthickloop}

\label{ex:R1delooping}
	The simplest form of delooping consists of removing the loop in the local picture
	\[
		
	\begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(current bounding box.center)]
		\pgfkeys{/diagramarcs, extra={\node at (0.2,0.4) {\tiny $u$};}}
		\begin{scope}
			\clip (0,0) circle (0.8cm);

			\fill[gray!15]
				(-0.5,0.65) .. controls (-0.4,0.5) and (135:0.6) .. (135:0.35)
				arc (135:225:0.35)
				.. controls (-135:0.6) and (-0.4,-0.5) .. (-0.5,-0.65)
				-- (-1, -1) -- (-1, 1) -- cycle;

			\fill[gray!15]
				($(45:0.35)$) .. controls +(45:0.6) and +(-45:0.6) .. ($(-45:0.35)$)
				arc (-45:45:0.35);
		\end{scope}

		\draw[dotted,thick] (0,0) circle (0.8cm);

		\draw[dotted, dash pattern=on 1pt off 1pt, line width=0.5pt] (-0.18,0) -- (0.0,0);

		\draw[thick]
			(-0.5,0.65) .. controls (-0.4,0.5) and (135:0.6) .. (135:0.35);
		\draw[thick]
			(-0.5,-0.65) .. controls (-0.4,-0.5) and (-135:0.6) .. (-135:0.35);
		\draw[thick]
			($(45:0.35)$)
			.. controls +(45:0.6) and +(-45:0.6)
			.. ($(-45:0.35)$);

		\pgfmathsetmacro{\Y}{int(mod(\arconedir+\arconeshade,2))}

		
	\begin{scope}[rotate=#3]

		\pgfmathsetmacro{\AAA}{int(abs((1-(#5)*(#5))*(100-(#5)*(#5))))}
		\pgfmathsetmacro{\BBB}{int(mod((#3)/90,2))}
		\pgfmathsetmacro{\CCC}{int(mod((#3)/90,4))}

		\ifnum \AAA>0
		\ifnum #2=0
		\ifnum #5>-3
		\fill[gray!15]
			($(135:0.35)$)
			.. controls +(-45:0.35*0.7) and +(45:0.35*0.7) .. ($(-135:0.35)$)
			arc (-135:-225:0.35);

		\fill[gray!15]
			($(-45:0.35)$)
			.. controls +(135:0.35*0.7) and +(-135:0.35*0.7) .. ($(45:0.35)$)

		\node[anchor=center] at (0.7,0.7) {\qdeg};

		=
		
	K_{R^\partial[u]}{\begin{pmatrix}
			0 & (x_0-u)(u+x_0+G)
		\end{pmatrix}
	}

		,
	\]
	where the boundary ring is $R^\partial=\mathbb{Z}[G,x_0]$. By simplifying with respect to $b_1(u)=(x_0-u)(u+x_0+G)$, we are left with the special deformation retract
	\begin{equation}\label{sdr:simpleloopremoval}
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6, ampersand replacement=\&]
		{\scalebox{0.8}{
	\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,baseline=(current bounding box.center)]
		\pgfkeys{/diagramarcs, q=1, hslash}

		\begin{pgfinterruptboundingbox}
			\begin{scope}
				\clip (0,0) circle (0.8cm);

				\fill[gray!15]
					(110:0.8)
					.. controls +(-45:0.6) and +(45:0.6) .. (-110:0.8)
					-- (-1.5, -1.2) -- (-1.5, 1.2) -- cycle;
			\end{scope}
		\end{pgfinterruptboundingbox}

		\draw[dotted,thick] (0,0) circle (0.8cm);

		\draw[thick]
			(110:0.8)
			.. controls +(-45:0.6) and +(45:0.6)
			.. (-110:0.8);

		\node[anchor=center] at (0.7,0.7) {\qdeg};
		\node[anchor=center] at (0.7,-0.7) {\hdeg};
		\node[anchor=center] at (-0.7,-0.7) {\hslashdeg};

		\extralines
	\end{tikzpicture}
}\oplus \scalebox{0.8}{
	\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,baseline=(current bounding box.center)]
		\pgfkeys{/diagramarcs, q=-1, hslash}

		\begin{pgfinterruptboundingbox}
			\begin{scope}
				\clip (0,0) circle (0.8cm);

				\fill[gray!15]

\label{ex:thinthin}
	Loops may appear in more complicated shapes, and there are different ways to deal with them. The diagram

 =
		
	K_{R^\partial[u]}{\begin{pmatrix}
			 x_0-x_1 & (x_0-u)(u+x_1+G) \\
			x_0-x_1 & (u+x_1-x_0-x_2)(u+x_2+G) 
		\end{pmatrix}
	}

	\]
	can be simplified by reducing with respect to the quadratic polynomial $b_2(u)=(u+x_1-x_0-x_2)(u+x_2+G)$.

<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
Let $\Gamma$ be a decorated diagram, and let $\Gamma'$ be an alteration as in Remark \ref{remarkalteration}. If we have an explicit special deformation retract simplifying the matrix factorization associated to a diagram $\Gamma$, then we can also obtain formulas for a simplification of $\Gamma'$. Indeed, the algebraic transformation rules extend to terms appearing in maps such as $\partial_j$, $P_f^{u,l}$ and $\operatorname{Quo}_f^u$: arc alterations affect $\partial_j$ the same way they do $\theta_j$, while coloring changes or edge relabelings alter $P_f^{u,l}$ and $\operatorname{Quo}_f^u$ by applying the corresponding variable transformations to $u$ and $f$.
<!-- end remark-4 -->

[5cm].
	\end{equation}
	Here, we are flipping the signs of $\theta_1$, $\partial_1$, $G$ and all the edge variables. The sign of the original $b_2(u)=(u+x_1-x_0-x_2)(u+x_2+G) $ did not change, and we used the fact that $P^{-u,0}_{b_2(u)}=P^{u,0}_{b_2(u)}$, $P^{-u,1}_{b_2(u)}=-P^{u,1}_{b_2(u)}$ and $\operatorname{Quo}^{-u}_{b_2(u)}=\operatorname{Quo}^{u}_{b_2(u)}$. A different way of obtaining such a special deformation retract is by applying \eqref{sdr:delooping} directly. These two possibilities differ by $u\mapsto-u$.

 =
		
	K_{R^\partial[u]}{\begin{pmatrix}
			x_0+x_2-x_1-x_3 & (u-x_4)(u+x_0+x_2+x_4-x_1-x_3+G) \\
			x_1-x_0 & (u+x_2-x_1-x_3)(u+x_0+x_2-x_3+G) \\
			x_2-x_3 & (x_3-u)(u+x_2+G)
		\end{pmatrix}
	}

		,
	\]
	there is no choice of arc $\alpha_j$ so that the terms $b_i$ in the second column fulfill the condition $P_{b_j}^{u,1}(b_i)=0$ for all $i\neq j$. Instead, we simplify this matrix factorization by first using an arc slide isomorphism

<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
First, do a sequence of arc removals to $\Gamma$ to obtain a deformation retract into a decorated diagram $\Gamma''$ with the same underlying 1-manifold, but with a minimal set of arcs. Now, given any loop in $\Gamma''$ not enclosing any other loops, it is always possible to do a sequence of arc slides to put it in the form appearing in Example \ref{ex:R1delooping}, and then apply the special deformation retract appearing there. This will create two summands with identical decorated diagrams with shifts $q\hslash$ and $q^{-1}\hslash$, respectively. The desired result is obtained by doing this process inductively, one loop at a time, with the number of summands doubling at each step. Observe that arc slide isomorphisms, arc removal deformation retracts and delooping special deformation retracts all admit explicit formulas, according to Lemma \ref{lem:arrowslide} and formulas \eqref{sdr:interiorfaceremoval} and \eqref{sdr:delooping}, respectively.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- definition-10: body_md -->
Let $R$ be a polynomial ring over $\mathbb{Z}$ graded by quantum degree $q$ and homological degree $h$, so that all polynomial generators live in degree $q^{-2}h^0$. A \emph{matrix multifactorization} over $R$ with potential $W\in R$ is a graded free $R$-module $M$ equipped with an additional $\mathbb{Z}/2$-grading $\hslash$ and with an $R$-module homomorphism $d_M$ of the form $d_M=\sum_{i=0}^\infty d_i$, with $d_i$ homogeneous of degree $q^{3(i-1)}h^i\hslash$, satisfying $d_M^2=W\cdot\mathrm{id}$. We require $M$ to be bounded in $h$-degree. We call $d_0$ the \emph{vertical component} of $d_M$, and we define the \emph{underlying} or \emph{vertical matrix factorization} of $M$ to be $\underline M=(M,d_0)$.
<!-- end definition-10 -->
<!-- definition-11: body_md -->
A \emph{decorated diagram} for an oriented tangle $T$ is an oriented tangle diagram $D$ in the 2-dimensional disk $\mathbb D^2$ together with
	\begin{itemize}
		\item a checkerboard coloring of the complement $\mathbb D^2\setminus D$,
		\item a finite indexed set $(\alpha_j)_{j\in I_{a}\subset \mathbb{N}}$ of non-intersecting directed arcs in the interior of the disk and away from the crossings, such that $\alpha_j\cap D=\partial \alpha_j$ transversally and $D\cup \bigcup_j\alpha_j$ is connected,
		\item a finite set $I_{c}\subseteq \mathbb{N}\setminus I_{a}$ indexing the crossings in $D$, and
		\item a possible choice of distinguished end in $\partial D$, in which case we may write $D^*$ to indicate the presence of such end. An asterisk $*$ may be placed near the distinguished end to graphically indicate it.
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end definition-11 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
Let us compare our conventions to the multifactorizations $M(-)$ associated to tangle diagrams in \cite{kwzmutation}. In \cite{kwzmutation}, tangles admit resolutions into webs, in which arcs have thickness rather than orientation, and edges are oriented and signed in compatible ways around each arc. The edge sign assignment should be thought of as a counterpart to our checkerboard colorings. Here is the correspondence of the matrix factorizations associated to elementary local pictures:

	and similarly for negative crossings. As a consequence of these identifications, if $D^*$ is a connected diagram for a tangle $T$ with boundary $E_*^4$, and $D^*_{\sigma_0}$ is the decorated tangle diagram obtained by coloring by $\sigma_0$, it follows that there is a vertical isomorphism $\mathcal M (D^*_{\sigma_0})\cong M(D^*)$ of matrix multifactorizations.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- definition-12: body_md -->
Let $E\subseteq \partial \mathbb D^2$ be finite and possibly based, and let $\partial\sigma$ be an alternating coloring of $\partial \mathbb D^2\setminus E$. Write $R^\partial$ and $W$ for the associated boundary ring and potential, respectively. For each $\Gamma\in Ob(\mathcal B(E))$, consider the checkerboard coloring $\sigma(\Gamma)$ compatible with $\partial\sigma$, and fix a minimal set of arcs, thus obtaining a decorated diagram $\widetilde \Gamma$. Denote the number of shaded components in $\mathbb D^2\setminus \Gamma$ by $|\sigma(\Gamma)|$. We define $\mathcal A(E,\partial\sigma)$ to be the full subcategory of $MF(R^\partial, W)$ generated by the matrix factorizations of the form $\mathcal M(\widetilde \Gamma)\hslash^{|\sigma(\Gamma)|}$, with $\Gamma$ running through all objects in $\mathcal B(E)$.
<!-- end definition-12 -->
<!-- definition-13: body_md -->
Let $(M,d)$ and $(M',d')$ be matrix multifactorizations. A \emph{special} $n$\emph{-deformation retract} from $M$ to $M'$ consists of 0-homomorphisms $\rho:M\to M'$ and $\iota:M'\to M$ such that $\rho\iota=1$, together with an $n$-homotopy $\kappa:M\to M$ between $\iota\rho$ and $1$ such that $\kappa\iota=0$, $\rho\kappa=0$ and $\kappa^2=0$. We often represent this data by a triple $(\iota,\rho,\kappa)$ or, more suggestively, by a diagram
	\[
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6,ampersand replacement=\&]
		{(M',d')} \arrow[r, "{\iota}", shift left=0.5ex,] \arrow[r, leftarrow, "{\rho}", shift right=0.5ex, swap] \&
		(M,d) \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{\kappa}",swap]
	\end{tikzcd} 

		.
	\]
<!-- end definition-13 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{prop:deloopmulti}
	Let $D$ be a decorated tangle diagram with boundary $E$ and coloring $\sigma$. Fix an integral lift of $\hslash$ enhancing $\mathcal A(E,\partial\sigma)$ into a dg-algebra. Then the multifactorization $\mathcal M (D)$ has an explicit special 0-deformation retract into an object $\mathcal M'(D)\in Tw^{h}\mathcal A(E,\partial\sigma)$.
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
The multifactorization associated to the decorated tangle diagram can be written as a cube of resolutions
	\[
		\mathcal M (D) = \bigoplus_{s\subseteq I_c} \mathcal M (\Gamma_s),
	\]
	where each summand is a matrix factorization associated to a decorated crossingless diagram. Then Proposition \ref{prop:mfdelooping} can be used to decompose each summand into shifted copies of the objects in $\mathcal A(E,\partial\sigma)$ up to special $0$-deformation retract, thus defining a special deformation retract of matrix factorizations
	\[
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6,ampersand replacement=\&]
		{\underline{\mathcal M}'} \arrow[r, "{}", shift left=0.5ex,] \arrow[r, leftarrow, "{}", shift right=0.5ex, swap] \&
		\underline{\mathcal M (D)} \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{}",swap]
	\end{tikzcd} 
.
	\]
	The desired multifactorization deformation retract $\mathcal M'(D)$ is obtained by altering $d_{\underline{\mathcal M}'}$ in the way indicated by the following lemma and its proof.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:specialDefRetract}
	Suppose
	\[
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6,ampersand replacement=\&]
		{(M',d')} \arrow[r, "{i}", shift left=0.5ex,] \arrow[r, leftarrow, "{p}", shift right=0.5ex, swap] \&
		(M,d) \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{k}",swap]
	\end{tikzcd} 

	\]
	is a special 1-deformation retract between matrix multifactorizations $M'$ and $M$, and let $\tilde d$ be another multifactorization differential in $M$ such that its vertical part coincides with $d$ (i.e. $\tilde d_0=d_0$) and $(\tilde d-d)k$ is nilpotent. Then there exists a special 1-deformation retract
	\[
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6,ampersand replacement=\&]
		{(M',\tilde d')} \arrow[r, "{\iota}", shift left=0.5ex,] \arrow[r, leftarrow, "{\rho}", shift right=0.5ex, swap] \&
		(M,\tilde d) \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{\kappa}",swap]
	\end{tikzcd} 
.
	\]
	such that $\tilde d'_0=d'_0$.
	If, furthermore, $k$ is a $0$-homotopy, making the original data a special 0-deformation retract, then the result is also a special 0-deformation retract, with the additional properties $\rho_0=p_0$, $\iota_0=i_0$ and $\kappa_0=k_0$.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Let
	\[
		A=(1-(\tilde d-d)k)^{-1}(\tilde d-d)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty} ((\tilde d-d)k)^j(\tilde d-d).
	\]
	Notice that $\tilde d-d$ has filtered degree $\geq 1$, while $k$ has filtered degree $\geq -1$. In total, $A$ has filtered degree $\geq 1$.
	Take
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:deformedsdrdata}
		\begin{aligned}
			\tilde d'&=d'+pAi
			\\
			\rho&=p+pAk
			\\
			\iota&=i+kAi
			\\
			\kappa&=k+kAk
		\end{aligned}
	\end{equation}
	These define special deformation retract data: see \cite{crainic} for calculations, which readily adapt to non-zero potential. Notice that the term $pAi$, i.e. the deformation of the differential, has filtered degree $\geq 1$, which guarantees $\tilde d_0'=d_0'$. In the case where $k$ is a 1-homotopy, then $pAk$ and $kAi$ preserve filtration, while $kAk$ has filtered degree $\geq -1$, which are necessary conditions for the deformed data $(\iota,\rho,\kappa)$ to give a special 1-deformation retract. If $k$ is additionally a 0-homotopy, then all these deformation terms have now filtered degree $\geq 1$, so that $(\iota,\rho,\kappa)$ is a special 0-deformation retract with $\rho_0=p_0$, $\iota_0=i_0$ and $\kappa_0=k_0$.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
Let $\mathbf k$ be a field. There is a $q$-filtered quasi-isomorphism
	\[
		\mathcal F: \mathcal A( E_*^4,\partial\sigma_0)\otimes \mathbf k \to \mathcal B^\infty_{ds}\otimes \mathbf k.
	\]
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- definition-14: body_md -->
Let $T$ be a tangle with boundary $E^4_*$, and let $D$ be a decorated tangle diagram for $T$ with coloring $\sigma_0$. Let $\mathcal M'(D)\in Tw^{h}\mathcal A(E^4_*,\partial\sigma_0)$ be a special 0-deformation retract of $\mathcal M(D)$. Define
	\[
		\text{\CYRD}^\infty(D) \in Tw^h (\mathcal B^\infty_{ds}\otimes\mathbf k)
	\]
	to be the image of $\mathcal M'(D)\otimes\mathbf k$ under the map induced by the quasi-isomorphism $\mathcal F$ at the level of bifiltered twisted complexes.
<!-- end definition-14 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:invariance}
	Let $T$ be an oriented tangle with boundary $E$ and let $D$ and $D'$ be decorated tangle diagrams for $T$ with fixed boundary coloring $\partial\sigma$. Then $\mathcal M(D)$ and $\mathcal M(D')$ are 1-homotopy equivalent matrix multifactorizations.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
Let $T$ be a tangle with boundary $E^4_*$, and let $D$ be a decorated tangle diagram for $T$ with coloring $\sigma_0$. Then the invariant $\text{\CYRD}^\infty(T):=\text{\CYRD}^\infty(D)\in Tw^h (\mathcal B^\infty_{ds}\otimes\mathbf k)$ is well defined up to 1-homotopy equivalence of bifiltered twisted complexes.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Given a decorated diagram $D$ for $T$, we have $\text{\CYRD}^\infty(T)=\mathcal F(\mathcal M'( D)\otimes\mathbf k)$, where $\mathcal M' (D)\in Tw^h (\mathcal A( E_*^4,\partial\sigma_0))$ is a special 0-deformation retract of $\mathcal M (D)$. A different choice of diagram $D'$ and special 0-deformation retract $\mathcal M' (D')$ will be 1-homotopy equivalent to $\mathcal M' (D)$, by Theorem \ref{thm:invariance}. Since $\mathcal F$ preserves 1-homotopy equivalence, the corollary follows.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
By the same arguments, there is another invariant of based 4-ended tangles corresponding to the reverse coloring $\sigma_1$. It contains the same information as $\text{\CYRD}^\infty(-)$.
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\label{prop:arcinvariance}
	Let $D$ and $D'$ be decorated tangle diagrams with the same underlying tangle diagram, coloring and distinguished end, if there is one.
	\begin{enumerate}
		\item Suppose $D$ and $D'$ differ only in their respective sets of arcs $A$ and $A'$, and $A\subset A'$. Then there is a
		special $0$-deformation retract of multifactorizations
		\[
			 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6,ampersand replacement=\&]
		{\mathcal M(D)} \arrow[r, "{}", shift left=0.5ex,] \arrow[r, leftarrow, "{}", shift right=0.5ex, swap] \&
		\mathcal M(D') \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{}",swap]
	\end{tikzcd} 
.
		\]
		\item Suppose $D$ and $D'$ differ only by a permutation of the
		indexing set $I=I_a\cup I_c$. Then there is a vertical isomorphism of matrix multifactorizations
		$\mathcal M(D)\cong\mathcal M(D')$.
		\item Suppose $D$ and $D'$ differ only in their respective sets of arcs $A$ and $A'$, but these are arbitrary. Then $\mathcal M(D)$ and $\mathcal M(D')$ are 0-homotopy equivalent.
	\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:concat}
	Suppose we have special $n$-deformation retracts
	\[
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6,ampersand replacement=\&]
		{(A,d_A)} \arrow[r, "{i_1}", shift left=0.5ex,] \arrow[r, leftarrow, "{p_1}", shift right=0.5ex, swap] \&
		(B,d_B) \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{k_1}",swap]
	\end{tikzcd} 

		,\qquad
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6,ampersand replacement=\&]
		{(B,d_B)} \arrow[r, "{i_2}", shift left=0.5ex,] \arrow[r, leftarrow, "{p_2}", shift right=0.5ex, swap] \&
		(C,d_C) \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{k_2}",swap]
	\end{tikzcd} 

		.
	\]
	Then the following is a special $n$-deformation retract
	\[
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6,ampersand replacement=\&]
		{(A,d_A)} \arrow[r, "{i_2i_1}", shift left=0.5ex,] \arrow[r, leftarrow, "{p_1p_2}", shift right=0.5ex, swap] \&
		(C,d_C) \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{k_2+i_2k_1p_2}",swap]
	\end{tikzcd} 

		.
	\]
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
For part 1, assume first that $A'=A\cup \{\alpha\}$. If we define vertical deformation retract data consisting of arc removal \eqref{sdr:interiorfaceremoval} at each resolution, the Perturbation Lemma \ref{lem:specialDefRetract} yields a special 0-deformation retract of the desired form. Such special 0-deformation retracts can be concatenated by Lemma \ref{lem:concat} to obtain the general case $A\subset A'$. Regarding part 2, we note that relabeling isomorphisms may be used at each resolution to define a vertical isomorphism of matrix multifactorizations. For the final part, assume first that there are no intersections between arcs in $A$ and $A'$. Then there is a third diagram $D''$ with arc system $A\cup A'$, up to some relabeling, so that $\mathcal M(D'')$ has both $\mathcal M(D)$ and $\mathcal M(D')$ as special 0-deformation retracts, making them 0-homotopy equivalent. For the general case, we can assume that the arcs in $A$ and $A'$ intersect transversally. Consider arcs $\alpha\in A$ and $\alpha'\in A'$ intersecting at $p\in\mathbb D^2$, and such that $\alpha'$ meets no other arcs in $A$ in the segment $\beta$ between $p$ and the tip of $\alpha'$. Consider arcs $\alpha_i$ and $\alpha_j$ which run parallel to $\alpha$ except in a neighborhood of the segment $\beta$, where they take the form
	\[
		\begin{tikzpicture}[scale=1.1, baseline={(0,0)}]
			\fill[black!10] (-35:1) .. controls (0.48,-0.42) and (0.48,0.42) .. (35:1)
				arc[start angle=35, end angle=-35, radius=1] -- cycle;
			\draw[line width=0.9pt] (-35:1) .. controls (0.48,-0.42) and (0.48,0.42) .. (35:1);
			\draw[dotted, line width=0.6pt] (0,0) circle (1);
			\draw[dotted, dash pattern=on 1pt off 1pt, line width=0.6pt] (-0.3,0.954) -- (-0.3,-0.954);
			\draw[dash pattern=on 2.5pt off 1.5pt, line width=0.5pt, black!55, -{>[scale=0.8]}] (-1,0) -- (0.565,0);
			\draw[dotted, dash pattern=on 1pt off 1pt, line width=0.6pt, -{>[scale=0.8]}] (-0.19,0.982) -- (-0.19,0.27)
				.. controls (-0.19,0.21) and (-0.17,0.19) .. (-0.11,0.19) -- (0.583,0.19);
			\draw[dotted, dash pattern=on 1pt off 1pt, line width=0.6pt, -{>[scale=0.8]}] (-0.19,-0.982) -- (-0.19,-0.27)
				.. controls (-0.19,-0.21) and (-0.17,-0.19) .. (-0.11,-0.19) -- (0.583,-0.19);
			\fill (-0.3,0) circle (0.7pt);
			\node[font=\tiny, anchor=north east, inner sep=1pt] at (-0.32,-0.02) {$p$};
			\node[font=\tiny, anchor=east, inner sep=1pt] at (-0.32,0.6) {$\alpha$};
			\node[font=\tiny, anchor=south, inner sep=1pt] at (-0.68,0.03) {$\alpha'$};
			\node[font=\tiny, anchor=west, inner sep=1pt] at (-0.17,0.62) {$\alpha_i$};
			\node[font=\tiny, anchor=west, inner sep=1pt] at (-0.17,-0.62) {$\alpha_j$};
		\end{tikzpicture}_.
	\]
	By part 1, $\mathcal M(D)$ is a special 0-deformation retract of the multifactorization $\mathcal M(\tilde D)$ associated to the diagram $\tilde D$ with arc system $A\cup\{\alpha_i,\alpha_j\}$. The latter, in turn, has another special 0-deformation retract $\mathcal M(\overline D)$ associated to the diagram $\overline D$ with arc system $\overline A:=A\cup\{\alpha_i,\alpha_j\}\setminus \{\alpha\}$. But now there is one fewer intersection point between arcs in $\overline A$ and $A'$ than between arcs in $A$ and $A'$. We can iterate this argument to reduce to the intersection-free case.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:SummandLemma}
	Let $(M,d)$ be a matrix multifactorization of the form $M=C\oplus M'$, with differential
	$d_M=\begin{pmatrix}d_C & \beta \\ \gamma & d_{M'} \end{pmatrix}$, and such that $\beta_0$ and $\gamma_0$ vanish. Suppose that we have a special 1-deformation retract
	\[
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6,ampersand replacement=\&]
		{0} \arrow[r, "{}", shift left=0.5ex,] \arrow[r, leftarrow, "{}", shift right=0.5ex, swap] \&
		(C,d_C) \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{k}",swap]
	\end{tikzcd} 
.
	\]
	Then there exists a special 1-deformation retract
	\[
		 
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6, ampersand replacement=\&]
		{(M',d_{M'}+\gamma k\beta)}
		\arrow[r, "{\iota}", shift left=0.5ex, color=ForestGreen]
		\arrow[r, leftarrow, "{\rho}", shift right=0.5ex, swap, color=ForestGreen]
		\& (M,d_M) \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{\kappa}",swap, color=ForestGreen]
	\end{tikzcd} 
.
	\]
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
There are special 1-deformation retracts
	\begin{align*}
		\emph{(R1a)} &

		\extralines
	\end{tikzpicture}
}} \arrow[r, "{}", shift left=0.5ex,] \arrow[r, leftarrow, "{}", shift right=0.5ex, swap] \&
		
	\begin{tikzpicture}[baseline=(current bounding box.center)]

				.. controls (-135:0.6) and (-0.4,-0.5) .. (-0.5,-0.65)

		\draw[thick]

		\pgfmathsetmacro{\Y}{int(mod(\arconedir+\arconeshade,2))}

	\end{scope}

		\node[anchor=center] at (0.7,0.7) {\qdeg};

 \arrow[loop right, distance=1em, in=15, out=-15, "{}",swap]
	\end{tikzcd} 

		&\emph{(R1b)}&  
	\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=#6,ampersand replacement=\&]
		{\scalebox{0.9}{
	\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,baseline=(current bounding box.center)]
		\pgfkeys{/diagramarcs, }

		\begin{pgfinterruptboundingbox}
			\begin{scope}

		\node[anchor=center] at (-0.7,-0.7) {\hslashdeg};

	There are also analogous special 1-deformation retracts if all colorings are inverted.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
We build explicit special 1-deformation retracts for each move, using the methods for diagram simplification in Subsection~\ref{subsec:simplify}.\footnote{All calculations in this proof can be verified by hand. For the reader's convenience, a Python notebook automating some of our constructions and carrying out these computations is available at \url{https://github.com/tme93/bar-natan-multifactorizations}.} Reidemeister 1 deformation retracts are illustrated in Figure \ref{fig:R1}. Let us first focus on the R1a move, which corresponds to the left side of the figure. The multifactorization initially associated to the tangle

	\]
	is at the bottom row of Figure \ref{fig:R1}. With the application of the Perturbation Lemma \ref{lem:specialDefRetract} in mind, let us denote this bottom row by $(C,D)$ and the middle row by $(C',D')$. The violet arrows represent the special deformation retracts of matrix factorizations appearing in Examples \ref{ex:R1arcremoval} and \ref{ex:R1delooping}. Together, these violet
	arrows define a special 0-deformation retract between the vertical factorizations $\underline C$ and $\underline C'$. In this case, the perturbation $D-d$ is the horizontal part $D_1$ consisting of just a saddle map. By Formula \eqref{eq:deformedsdrdata}, the only non-vertical component in $(C',D')$ is calculated as the composite of the other three labeled arrows.

	Now, in the middle row $(C',D')$ we have a 1-contractible summand
	\[
		\left[\begin{tikzcd}
			\scalebox{0.6}{
	\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,baseline=(current bounding box.center)]
		\pgfkeys{/diagramarcs, q=2}

					-- (-1.5, -1.2) -- (-1.5, 1.2) -- cycle;
			\end{scope}
		\end{pgfinterruptboundingbox}

		\draw[dotted,thick] (0,0) circle (0.8cm);

		\node[anchor=center] at (-0.7,-0.7) {\hslashdeg};

}
			\arrow[r,"1"] &
			\scalebox{0.6}{
	\begin{tikzpicture}[thick,baseline=(current bounding box.center)]

		\end{pgfinterruptboundingbox}

		\node[anchor=center] at (-0.7,-0.7) {\hslashdeg};

		\extralines
	\end{tikzpicture}
}
		\end{tikzcd}\right]_,
	\]
	which we cancel according to Lemma \ref{lem:SummandLemma}. The R1b move is completely analogous.\\

				\arrow[ddd, from=4-2, to=7-2, bend right=40, crossing over, color=violet, shift right=1.5, "{\begin{pmatrix}
						1 & u
				\end{pmatrix}}(-\theta_1+\theta_2)", swap, pos=0.68]
				\arrow[uuu, from=7-2, to=4-2, bend left=40, crossing over, color=violet, shift right=1.5, "{\begin{pmatrix}P^{u,0}_{(u+x_1-x_0-x_2)(u+x_2+G)} \\ P^{u,1}_{(u+x_1-x_0-x_2)(u+x_2+G)}\end{pmatrix}\partial_2}", swap, pos=0]
			\end{tikzcd}
		\]
		\caption{Invariance under R2a move.}
		\label{fig:r2}
	\end{figure}

	appears at the bottom layer. There is, again, a special deformation retract simplifying the decorated diagram at each vertex, depicted in violet. A couple of these deformation retracts were explicitly computed in Examples \ref{ex:R2arcremoval} and \ref{ex:thinthin}, while the others are computed analogously. Following the same perturbation argument that we used for R1a, we obtain all the arrows in the middle layer from the given vertical special deformation retract data via the Perturbation Lemma \ref{lem:specialDefRetract}. Observe that, in principle, Formula \eqref{eq:deformedsdrdata} could give an arrow of length (i.e. $h$-degree) 2, but it vanishes by direct calculation. We then use Lemma \ref{lem:SummandLemma} twice to remove first the 1-contractible piece corresponding to the isomorphism $-(\partial_1\theta_1+\theta_2\partial_1)$ with target at the rightmost vertex, and then the 1-contractible piece corresponding to the isomorphism $-1$ with source the leftmost vertex.

	The R2b move corresponds to an orientation reversal on both strands of R2a. To obtain the desired special 1-deformation retract in that case, we simply reverse all the arcs in Figure \ref{fig:r2}.\\

		\caption{Intermediate simplification steps in the proof of Reidemeister 3: $M_{R3}'$ (top) and $M_{R3}''$ (bottom). In $M_{R3}''$ we have $f:=1+(\partial_2 +(x_1-x_2)\theta_2)\theta_1$, $g_2:=1+(x_4-x_3)\theta_1\theta_2+(\theta_1+\theta_2)\partial_3$ and $g_1:=\partial_3(1-\partial_2\theta_3)+(x_1-x_0)\theta_2\partial_3\theta_3-(x_0+x_2+x_4-x_1+G)\theta_3$.}
		\label{fig:r3}
	\end{figure}

	Observe that, by Proposition \ref{prop:arcinvariance}, no generality is lost after the relabeling on the right-hand side. Let $M_{R3}$ be the multifactorization associated to the left-hand side diagram. We simplify in three steps.

	\emph{Step 1.} Consider the multifactorization $M_{R3}'$ at the top of Figure \ref{fig:r3}, which is obtained from $M_{R3}$ by relabeling arcs at the vertices $110$ and $111$ of the cube, while also sliding an arc at $101$. We have corresponding explicit matrix factorization isomorphisms $\tau_{1,3}$, $\tau_{2,3}$ and $\psi_{2,3}$ for the transformations at each of these three vertices, afforded by Lemmas \ref{lem:labelperm} and \ref{lem:arrowslide}. The components of $(d_{M'_{R3}})_1$ are defined by compositions of the original saddles $S_i$ with the corresponding isomorphisms and their inverses as needed. There is a vertical isomorphism $M_{R3}\cong M'_{R3}$ given by the chosen isomorphisms at the vertices $110$, $111$ and $101$, and identities at all other vertices. These particular transformations are chosen to help obtain a conveniently symmetric form in the fully simplified expression $M_{R3}'''$ below.

	\emph{Step 2.} Next, we simplify all diagrams by removing an arc at each resolution other than the one sitting at the vertex $101$, in which we do a delooping. Explicit formulas for the special deformation retracts at $000$ and $101$ can be found in Examples \ref{ex:R3arcremoval} and \ref{ex:circleremovalreid3}, respectively. At all other vertices, the special deformation retract is computed just as in $000$. This defines a vertical special deformation retract $\underline{M''_{R3}}$ of $\underline{M'_{R3}}$. Through the Perturbation Lemma \ref{lem:specialDefRetract} and the formulas in its proof, we obtain the special 0-deformation retract $M''_{R3}$ of $M'_{R3}$. All the explicit information we need from $M''_{R3}$ appears at the bottom of Figure~\ref{fig:r3}. There are possible length 2 and 3 arrows ending at $111$, but they are not depicted as they disappear without contribution in the next step. The same goes for the depicted but unlabeled arrows and arrow components.

		\caption{Simplified matrix factorizations $M_{R3}'''$ (top) and $N_{R3}'''$ (bottom) for the two sides of Reidemeister 3. }
		\label{fig:r3simp}
	\end{figure}

	\emph{Step 3.} Finally, we remove 1-contractible pieces using 1-nullhomotopies indicated by the green arrows in Figure~\ref{fig:r3}. The component $f=1+(\partial_2 +(x_1-x_2)\theta_2)\theta_1$ of the arrow $101\to 111$ in $M''_{R3}$ has inverse $f^{-1}:=1-(\partial_2 +(x_1-x_2)\theta_2)\theta_1$. Applying Lemma \ref{lem:SummandLemma}, we obtain a special 1-deformation retract of $M''_{R3}$ in which the first summand at 101 and the entire 111 piece are removed, while all the differentials between the remaining pieces remain untouched. The same lemma can be applied again to the $-1$ component of $100\to 101$, this time creating new arrows along $000\to 110$ and $001\to 110$. The multifactorization $M'''_{R3}$ obtained after these two cancellations appears at the top of Figure \ref{fig:r3simp}.

	Now, the two decorated tangle diagrams in \eqref{eq:R3} are related by a $180^\circ$ rotation, followed by a coloring inversion and a swap of all strand orientations. Let us denote by $N_{R3}$ the multifactorization associated to the right-hand side in \eqref{eq:R3}. The vertical part $\underline N_{R3}$ is obtained from $\underline M_{R3}$ by doing $180^\circ$ rotation, coloring inversion and reversal of arc directions at each resolved diagram. On the terms defining length 1 and 2 arrows, the total transformation has the following effect:
	\[
		x_0\leftrightarrow -x_3, \quad x_1\leftrightarrow -x_4, \quad x_2\leftrightarrow -x_5, \quad u\leftrightarrow -u, \quad G\leftrightarrow -G.
	\]
	These transformations can be carried throughout the special deformation retracts, using the algebraic recipe, to transform arrows $d_k$ of length $k>0$. Applying this total transformation to $M_{R3}'''$, one obtains the multifactorization $N_{R3}'''$ at the bottom of Figure \ref{fig:r3simp}. These two simplified multifactorizations are related by exchanging labels everywhere and then flipping the sign of all differentials. More precisely, $M'''_{R3}\cong N_{R3}'''$ by a vertical isomorphism which has a component of the form $s_2s_3\tau_{2,3}$ between each decorated diagram in $M'''_{R3}$ and the corresponding decorated diagram in $N'''_{R3}$ with the same underlying crossingless matching.
